## Dialogues

- `gs-morning` (getting-started): **Good morning**. Two neighbours greet each other in the morning.
  - Bùkọ́lá (female): Ẹ káàárọ̀. (Good morning.)
  - Adé (male): Ẹ káàárọ̀. Ṣé àlàáfíà ni? (Good morning. Is everything well?)
  - Bùkọ́lá (female): Àlàáfíà ni. Ẹ ṣé. (Everything is well. Thank you.)
  - Adé (male): Ẹ ṣé. Ó dàbọ̀. (Thank you. Goodbye.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "Ṣé àlàáfíà ni?" and "Àlàáfíà ni" (the usual how-are-you exchange) and the tones on "Ó dàbọ̀". All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `gs-evening` (getting-started): **Good evening**. Two neighbours meet in the evening after work.
  - Bùkọ́lá (female): Ẹ káalẹ́, Adé. (Good evening, Adé.)
  - Adé (male): Ẹ káalẹ́, Bùkọ́lá. Ẹ kúuṣẹ́. (Good evening, Bùkọ́lá. Well done with your work.)
  - Bùkọ́lá (female): Ẹ ṣé. Ṣé àlàáfíà ni? (Thank you. Is everything well?)
  - Adé (male): Àlàáfíà ni. (Everything is well.)
  - Bùkọ́lá (female): Ó dàbọ̀. (Goodbye.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "Ẹ káalẹ́" and "Ẹ kúuṣẹ́" against Getting Started 2 (Ẹ kúuṣẹ́ is no longer taught in a lesson), and the tone on "Ṣé". All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `gs-names` (getting-started): **What is your name?**. Two people meet for the first time.
  - Adé (male): Ẹ káàárọ̀. Orúkọ mi ni Adé. (Good morning. My name is Adé.)
  - Bùkọ́lá (female): Ẹ káàárọ̀, Adé. Orúkọ mi ni Bùkọ́lá. (Good morning, Adé. My name is Bùkọ́lá.)
  - Adé (male): Inú mi dùn. (I am glad to meet you.)
  - Bùkọ́lá (female): Inú mi dùn pẹ̀lú. (I am glad too.)
  - Adé (male): Ọmọ Ìbàdàn ni mí. (I am from Ibadan.)
  - Bùkọ́lá (female): Ọmọ Èkó ni mí. (I am from Lagos.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "Inú mi dùn" as "glad to meet you", and "Ọmọ Ìbàdàn ni mí" and "Ọmọ Èkó ni mí" (literally "I am a child of Ibadan or Lagos"). All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `b-fish` (beginner): **Buying fish**. A man buys fish from a woman at her market stall.
  - Ṣọlá (male): Ẹ káàárọ̀, ìyá. (Good morning, mother.)
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Ẹ káàárọ̀. Kí ni ẹ fẹ́? (Good morning. What do you want?)
  - Ṣọlá (male): Mo fẹ́ ra ẹja. (I want to buy fish.)
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Ẹja yìí dára. (This fish is good.)
  - Ṣọlá (male): Èló ni ẹja yìí? (How much is this fish?)
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Àádọ́ta náírà. (Fifty naira.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "Èló ni?" for how much, "ìyá" as a respectful word for an older woman, and "Àádọ́ta" (50) against the Numbers section. All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `b-eggs` (beginner): **I do not want that**. The same man says what he does not want.
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Ṣé ẹ fẹ́ ata? (Do you want pepper?)
  - Ṣọlá (male): Rárá, mi ò fẹ́ ata. (No, I do not want pepper.)
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Ṣé ẹ fẹ́ ẹran? (Do you want meat?)
  - Ṣọlá (male): Mi ò fẹ́ ẹran. Mo fẹ́ ẹyin. (I do not want meat. I want eggs.)
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Ẹyin mélòó? (How many eggs?)
  - Ṣọlá (male): Ẹyin mẹ́ta. (Three eggs.)
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Ó dára. (Good.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "mi ò fẹ́" (I do not want), "Rárá" (no) and "Ẹyin mélòó" (how many eggs). All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `b-price` (beginner): **Talking about the price**. He thinks the fish costs too much and asks for less.
  - Ṣọlá (male): Ẹja yìí wọ́n jù. (This fish is too expensive.)
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Rárá o, ẹja yìí dára gan-an. (No, this fish is very good.)
  - Ṣọlá (male): Ṣé ẹ lè dín owó kù? (Can you lower the price?)
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Mo lè dín kù. Ogójì náírà. (I can lower it. Forty naira.)
  - Ṣọlá (male): Ó dára. Mo ń san owó. (Good. I am paying.)
  - Ìyá Ọlá (female): Ẹ ṣé. Ó dàbọ̀. (Thank you. Goodbye.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "wọ́n jù" (too expensive), "dín owó kù" (lower the price), "gan-an" and "Ogójì" (40) against the Numbers section. All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `i-going` (intermediate): **Where are you going?**. Two people wait by the road.
  - Dàpọ̀ (male): Ẹ pẹ̀lẹ́. Ibo ni ẹ ń lọ? (Hello. Where are you going?)
  - Kẹ́mi (female): Mo ń lọ sí ọjà. (I am going to the market.)
  - Dàpọ̀ (male): Èmi náà máa lọ síbẹ̀. (I will go there too.)
  - Kẹ́mi (female): Ṣé ẹ ti rí ọkọ̀? (Have you got a vehicle yet?)
  - Dàpọ̀ (male): Rárá, mi ò tíì rí ọkọ̀. (No, I have not got one yet.)
  - Kẹ́mi (female): Ọkọ̀ yóò dé láìpẹ́. (A vehicle will arrive soon.)
  - Dàpọ̀ (male): Ẹ ṣé. (Thank you.)
  - Kẹ́mi (female): Kò sí wàhálà. (No problem.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "Ẹ pẹ̀lẹ́" as a greeting, "mi ò tíì" (not yet), "láìpẹ́" (soon) and "Kò sí wàhálà" (no problem). All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `i-way` (intermediate): **Asking the way**. A man asks a woman how to get to the hospital.
  - Dàpọ̀ (male): Ẹ jọ̀wọ́, ibo ni ilé ìwòsàn wà? (Please, where is the hospital?)
  - Kẹ́mi (female): Ilé ìwòsàn náà wà nítòsí. (The hospital is nearby.)
  - Dàpọ̀ (male): Ṣé ó jìnnà? (Is it far?)
  - Kẹ́mi (female): Rárá, kò jìnnà. (No, it is not far.)
  - Dàpọ̀ (male): Ọ̀nà wo ni mo máa tọ̀? (Which road will I take?)
  - Kẹ́mi (female): Ẹ tọ ọ̀nà yìí lọ. Ẹ ó rí i lẹ́yìn ilé ìwé. (Follow this road. You will see it behind the school.)
  - Dàpọ̀ (male): Ẹ ṣé gan-an. (Thank you very much.)
  - Kẹ́mi (female): Ó dàbọ̀. (Goodbye.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "nítòsí" (nearby), "tọ ọ̀nà yìí lọ" (follow this road), "Ẹ ó rí i" (you will see it) and "lẹ́yìn" (behind). All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `i-bus` (intermediate): **Taking a bus**. A man gets on a bus and pays the fare.
  - Dàpọ̀ (male): Ẹ jọ̀wọ́, ṣé ọkọ̀ yìí ń lọ sí Ìkẹjà? (Please, is this bus going to Ikeja?)
  - Kẹ́mi (female): Bẹ́ẹ̀ ni. Ó ń lọ sí Ìkẹjà. (Yes. It is going to Ikeja.)
  - Dàpọ̀ (male): Èló ni owó ọkọ̀? (How much is the fare?)
  - Kẹ́mi (female): Ọgọ́rùn-ún náírà. (One hundred naira.)
  - Dàpọ̀ (male): Mo ní igba náírà. (I have two hundred naira.)
  - Kẹ́mi (female): Mo máa dá ọgọ́rùn-ún padà. (I will give back one hundred.)
  - Dàpọ̀ (male): Ọkọ̀ yóò dé Ìkẹjà láìpẹ́? (Will the bus reach Ikeja soon?)
  - Kẹ́mi (female): Bẹ́ẹ̀ ni, ó máa dé láìpẹ́. (Yes, it will arrive soon.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "owó ọkọ̀" (the fare), "Ọgọ́rùn-ún" (100) and "igba" (200) against the Numbers section, and "dá ... padà" (give back). All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `a-welcome` (advanced): **Welcome**. A guest arrives at his friend's house.
  - Ṣẹ́gun (male): Ẹ kú ilé o. (Greetings to the house.)
  - Tọ́pẹ́ (female): Ẹ káàbọ̀. Ẹ wọlé. (Welcome. Come in.)
  - Ṣẹ́gun (male): Ẹ ṣé. Ilé yìí dára gan-an. (Thank you. This house is very nice.)
  - Tọ́pẹ́ (female): Ẹ jókòó. Ẹ máa bá wa jẹun. (Sit down. Please eat with us.)
  - Ṣẹ́gun (male): Kí ni ẹ ń sè? (What are you cooking?)
  - Tọ́pẹ́ (female): Mo ń sè ọbẹ̀ tí ẹ fẹ́. (I am cooking the soup that you like.)
  - Ṣẹ́gun (male): Ẹ ṣé. Inú mi dùn gan-an. (Thank you. I am very happy.)
  - Tọ́pẹ́ (female): Oúnjẹ ti ṣe. Ẹ jẹun. (The food is ready. Eat.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "Ẹ kú ilé", "Ẹ káàbọ̀", "ọbẹ̀ tí ẹ fẹ́" (the soup that you like; a relative clause) and "Oúnjẹ ti ṣe". All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `a-family` (advanced): **Talking about family**. Over the meal, the two friends talk about her children.
  - Ṣẹ́gun (male): Ṣé ẹ ní ọmọ? (Do you have children?)
  - Tọ́pẹ́ (female): Mo ní ọmọ méjì. Ọmọkùnrin kan àti ọmọbìnrin kan. (I have two children. One boy and one girl.)
  - Ṣẹ́gun (male): Ọmọ wo ni ó dàgbà jù? (Which child is older?)
  - Tọ́pẹ́ (female): Ọmọbìnrin tí ó ń kàwé ni ó dàgbà jù. (It is the girl who is studying who is older.)
  - Ṣẹ́gun (male): Kíkàwé dára gan-an. (Studying is very good.)
  - Tọ́pẹ́ (female): Bẹ́ẹ̀ ni. Ó fẹ́ràn kíkàwé. (Yes. She loves studying.)
  - Ṣẹ́gun (male): Ṣé ọmọkùnrin náà ń lọ ilé ìwé? (Does the boy go to school too?)
  - Tọ́pẹ́ (female): Rárá, kò tíì dàgbà tó. (No, he is not old enough yet.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "ọmọkùnrin" and "ọmọbìnrin", "dàgbà jù" (older), "Kíkàwé" (studying, a verbal noun), "ọmọbìnrin tí ó ń kàwé" (a relative clause) and "kò tíì dàgbà tó". All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `a-goodbye` (advanced): **Saying goodbye**. The guest leaves after the meal.
  - Ṣẹ́gun (male): Mo ti jẹun yó. Ẹ ṣé gan-an. (I have eaten my fill. Thank you very much.)
  - Tọ́pẹ́ (female): Ẹ máa wá lẹ́ẹ̀kan sí i. (Please come again.)
  - Ṣẹ́gun (male): N óo wá lọ́sẹ̀ tó ń bọ̀. (I will come next week.)
  - Tọ́pẹ́ (female): Ẹ mú ọbẹ̀ yìí lọ sílé. (Take this soup home.)
  - Ṣẹ́gun (male): Ẹ ṣé o. Mo máa gbé e lọ. (Thank you. I will carry it home.)
  - Tọ́pẹ́ (female): Ẹ ṣọ́ra lọ́nà o. (Be careful on the road.)
  - Ṣẹ́gun (male): Ẹ ṣé. Ó dàbọ̀. (Thank you. Goodbye.)
  - Tọ́pẹ́ (female): Ó dàbọ̀. (Goodbye.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "yó" (full), "lẹ́ẹ̀kan sí i" (once more), "N óo wá" (I will come), "lọ́sẹ̀ tó ń bọ̀" (next week) and the serial verbs "mú ... lọ" and "gbé ... lọ". All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `m-advice` (mastery): **Asking for advice**. A young woman asks an elder what to do when work is hard.
  - Ìyábọ̀ (female): Ẹ káàárọ̀, bàbá. Ẹ jọ̀wọ́, mo fẹ́ gba ìmọ̀ràn lọ́wọ́ yín. (Good morning, sir. Please, I would like to take your advice.)
  - Bàbá Àgbà (male): Ẹ káàárọ̀, ọmọ mi. Kí ni ó ń dà ọ́ láàmú? (Good morning, my child. What is troubling you?)
  - Ìyábọ̀ (female): Iṣẹ́ mi le gan-an, mo sì ti rẹ̀. (My work is very hard, and I am tired.)
  - Bàbá Àgbà (male): Ààrò kì í gbóná kalẹ́, ọmọ mi. (The hearth does not stay hot until nighttime, my child.)
  - Ìyábọ̀ (female): Ṣé ẹ ń sọ pé ìṣòro yóò parí? (Are you saying the trouble will end?)
  - Bàbá Àgbà (male): Bẹ́ẹ̀ ni. Bí o bá ṣe sùúrù, ìgbà ayọ̀ yóò dé. (Yes. If you are patient, a time of joy will come.)
  - Ìyábọ̀ (female): Ẹ ṣé, bàbá. Ọkàn mi ti balẹ̀. (Thank you, sir. My heart is at peace.)
  - Bàbá Àgbà (male): Ọlọ́run yóò bá ọ lọ. (God will go with you.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: The proverb "Ààrò kì í gbóná kalẹ́" is in the Proverbs section (Wiktionary, needs-review). Check "ń dà ọ́ láàmú" (is troubling you), "ṣe sùúrù" (be patient) and "ọkàn mi ti balẹ̀". All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `m-learning` (mastery): **Learning from the old**. The elder talks about who taught him, and why he taught his children.
  - Ìyábọ̀ (female): Bàbá, ta ni ó kọ́ yín ní iṣẹ́ àgbẹ̀? (Sir, who taught you farming?)
  - Bàbá Àgbà (male): Bàbá mi ni ó kọ́ mi. (It was my father who taught me.)
  - Ìyábọ̀ (female): Ṣé ẹ kọ́ àwọn ọmọ yín náà? (Did you teach your own children too?)
  - Bàbá Àgbà (male): Mo kọ́ wọn, nítorí pé ọmọ tí a kò kọ́ ni yóò gbé ilé tí a kọ́ tà. (I taught them, because it is the child who is not taught who will sell the house that was built.)
  - Ìyábọ̀ (female): Òwe yìí jinlẹ̀ gan-an. (This proverb is very deep.)
  - Bàbá Àgbà (male): Bí o bá fetí sílẹ̀, o máa gbọ́n. (If you listen, you will become wise.)
  - Ìyábọ̀ (female): Mo ń fetí sílẹ̀, bàbá. (I am listening, sir.)
  - Bàbá Àgbà (male): Ìgbà yẹn ni ọgbọ́n bẹ̀rẹ̀. (That is when wisdom begins.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: The proverb "Ọmọ tí a kò kọ́ ni yóò gbé ilé tí a kọ́ tà" is taught in lesson m-4. Check "ta ni ó kọ́ yín" (focus questions), "fetí sílẹ̀" (listen) and "Ìgbà yẹn ni ọgbọ́n bẹ̀rẹ̀" (a focus sentence). All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.
- `m-thanks` (mastery): **Giving thanks**. She returns to thank him.
  - Ìyábọ̀ (female): Bàbá, mo wá dúpẹ́ lọ́wọ́ yín. (Sir, I have come to thank you.)
  - Bàbá Àgbà (male): Kí ló dé, ọmọ mi? (What is it, my child?)
  - Ìyábọ̀ (female): Nítorí pé ọ̀rọ̀ yín ràn mí lọ́wọ́. (Because your words helped me.)
  - Bàbá Àgbà (male): Ìwọ ni o ṣiṣẹ́, kì í ṣe èmi. (It is you who did the work, not me.)
  - Ìyábọ̀ (female): Bí ẹ kò bá gbà mí nímọ̀ràn, ǹ bá ti juwọ́ sílẹ̀. (If you had not advised me, I would have given up.)
  - Bàbá Àgbà (male): Ó dára. Ọmọ rere ni ọ́. (It is good. You are a good child.)
  - Ìyábọ̀ (female): Mo máa wá lẹ́ẹ̀kan sí i lọ́sẹ̀ tó ń bọ̀. (I will come again next week.)
  - Bàbá Àgbà (male): Wá nígbàkúùgbà tí o bá fẹ́. (Come whenever you like.)
  - Source: Written by the author of this code for this site, using words and patterns from the level's lessons; no published source.
  - Doubt: Check "dúpẹ́ lọ́wọ́" (give thanks to), "kì í ṣe èmi" (it is not me), the "bí ... bá ... ǹ bá ti" (if ... would have) sentence, and "nígbàkúùgbà" (whenever). All of this Yoruba was written by the author with no published source, so every line needs a native speaker.

## All learner-facing Yoruba

Every lesson's common mistake and practice questions, so a native speaker can review all Yoruba a learner sees.

### Getting Started

#### Sounds and tones (`gs-1`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: omo
- Right: ọmọ
- Why: Without the dots the letters are different sounds. Writing omo for ọmọ is a spelling error, not a style choice.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): gbọ́ is spelled with three letters (g, b and ọ), but it has ___ sounds.
  - Choices: 2 | 3 | 1
  - Answer: 2
- Q2 (pick): Which word starts with the vowel sound in "bed"?
  - Choices: ọmọ | ẹja | gbọ́
  - Answer: ẹja
- Q3 (pick): How is ṣ pronounced?
  - Choices: Like the "sh" in "ship" | Like the "s" in "sun" | Like the "ch" in "chin"
  - Answer: Like the "sh" in "ship"
- Q4 (pick): Which letter is said like the "aw" in "law"?
  - Choices: ọ | o | ẹ
  - Answer: ọ
- Q5 (blank): No mark on a syllable means ___ tone.
  - Choices: mid | high | low
  - Answer: mid
- Q6 (blank): In ọkọ̀, the last syllable has ___ tone.
  - Choices: low | high | mid
  - Answer: low
- Q7 (pick): ọkọ and ọkọ̀ have the same letters. What makes them different words?
  - Choices: The tone mark on the last syllable | The dots under the vowels | Nothing, they are the same word
  - Answer: The tone mark on the last syllable
- Q8 (pick): Which mark means the voice goes low?
  - Choices: ò | ó | o
  - Answer: ò

#### Greetings and your name (`gs-2`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Mi orúkọ ni Bùkọ́lá
- Right: Orúkọ mi ni Bùkọ́lá
- Why: English puts "my" first, but Yoruba puts mi after the noun: orúkọ mi.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Good morning (to an elder): Ẹ ___.
  - Choices: káàárọ̀ | kú àárọ̀ | kúàárọ̀
  - Answer: káàárọ̀
- Q2 (blank): kú + alẹ́ (evening) join in speech. Good evening (to an elder): Ẹ ___.
  - Choices: káalẹ́ | kúalẹ́ | kaalẹ́
  - Answer: káalẹ́
- Q3 (blank): Orúkọ ___ ni Bùkọ́lá. (My name is Bùkọ́lá.)
  - Choices: mi | rẹ | wa
  - Answer: mi
- Q4 (blank): ___ ni orúkọ rẹ? (What is your name?)
  - Choices: Kí | Báwo | Mo
  - Answer: Kí
- Q5 (blank): Someone gives you a gift. You say: Ẹ ___. (Thank you.)
  - Choices: ṣé | kú | mi
  - Answer: ṣé
- Q6 (pick): kú àárọ̀ becomes káàárọ̀. What happens to the u of kú?
  - Choices: It becomes a, matching the next vowel | It is dropped | It becomes i
  - Answer: It becomes a, matching the next vowel
- Q7 (pick): Who is ẹ káàárọ̀ for?
  - Choices: An older person or a group | Only your own age mates | Only a baby
  - Answer: An older person or a group
- Q8 (order): My name is Bùkọ́lá
  - Answer: Orúkọ mi ni Bùkọ́lá

#### Where you are from (`gs-3`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Wọ́n àti ẹ̀yin
- Right: Àwọn àti ẹ̀yin
- Why: They and you. àti means "and". A short form like wọ́n only goes right before a verb. Here no verb follows, so use the long form àwọn.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): ___ wá láti Nàìjíríà. (I come from Nigeria.)
  - Choices: Mo | Ó | A
  - Answer: Mo
- Q2 (blank): ___ wo ni o ti wá? (Which town are you from?)
  - Choices: Ìlú | Ìpínlẹ̀ | Orílẹ̀-èdè
  - Answer: Ìlú
- Q3 (blank): Ìlú ___ ni Ìbàdàn. (My town is Ibadan.)
  - Choices: mi | wo | wá
  - Answer: mi
- Q4 (pick): In Ìlú wo ni o ti wá?, where does wo (which) go?
  - Choices: Right after the kind of place | At the very end | Before the kind of place
  - Answer: Right after the kind of place
- Q5 (pick): Which pronoun do you use to speak politely to one older person?
  - Choices: o | ó | ẹ
  - Answer: ẹ
- Q6 (pick): In Àwọn àti ẹ̀yin (they and you), why are the long forms used?
  - Choices: No verb follows, so a short form cannot be used | Long forms are more polite | Short forms are only for children
  - Answer: No verb follows, so a short form cannot be used
- Q7 (order): Which town are you from?
  - Answer: Ìlú wo ni o ti wá
- Q8 (order): My state is Oyo
  - Answer: Ìpínlẹ̀ mi ni Ọ̀yọ́

#### Family and interests (`gs-4`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Èyí ni mi ìyá
- Right: Èyí ni ìyá mi
- Why: mi goes after the noun it belongs to: ìyá mi, not mi ìyá.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Èyí ni ìyá ___. (This is my mother.)
  - Choices: mi | rẹ | wa
  - Answer: mi
- Q2 (blank): Mo ___ bọ́ọ̀lù. (I like football.)
  - Choices: fẹ́ràn | ní | wá
  - Answer: fẹ́ràn
- Q3 (blank): Mo ní arábìnrin ___. (I have two sisters.)
  - Choices: méjì | kan | mẹ́ta
  - Answer: méjì
- Q4 (blank): Kí ni o ___? (What do you like?)
  - Choices: fẹ́ràn | ní | wá
  - Answer: fẹ́ràn
- Q5 (blank): Mo fẹ́ràn orin ___ ijó. (I like music and dancing.)
  - Choices: àti | láti | ni
  - Answer: àti
- Q6 (pick): Where does the number go in Mo ní arábìnrin méjì?
  - Choices: After the noun | Before the noun | Before Mo
  - Answer: After the noun
- Q7 (order): I like football and food
  - Answer: Mo fẹ́ràn bọ́ọ̀lù àti oúnjẹ
- Q8 (order): What do you like?
  - Answer: Kí ni o fẹ́ràn

### Beginner

#### "To be": jẹ́ and ni (`b-1`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Ó ni olùkọ́
- Right: Ó jẹ́ olùkọ́
- Why: COERLL marks Ó ni olùkọ́ as incorrect, because ni does not follow a short pronoun like ó. To say what someone is, use jẹ́: Ó jẹ́ olùkọ́, he is a teacher.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Mo ___ olùkọ́. (I am a teacher.)
  - Choices: jẹ́ | ni | àti
  - Answer: jẹ́
- Q2 (pick): Why is Ó ni olùkọ́ wrong?
  - Choices: ni does not follow a short pronoun like ó | ó is not a pronoun | olùkọ́ cannot follow ni
  - Answer: ni does not follow a short pronoun like ó
- Q3 (blank): ___ ni ọ̀rẹ́ mi. (He is my friend.)
  - Choices: Òun | Ó | Èmi
  - Answer: Òun
- Q4 (blank): Mo jẹ́ ___. (I am a doctor.)
  - Choices: dókítà | nọ́ọ̀sì | àgbẹ̀
  - Answer: dókítà
- Q5 (blank): Iṣẹ́ mi ___ olùkọ́. (My job is teacher.)
  - Choices: ni | jẹ́ | láti
  - Answer: ni
- Q6 (order): I am a doctor
  - Answer: Mo jẹ́ dókítà
- Q7 (order): My job is teacher
  - Answer: Iṣẹ́ mi ni olùkọ́
- Q8 (order): We are friends
  - Answer: Àwa jẹ́ ọ̀rẹ́

#### Negation with kò (`b-2`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Mo kò lọ
- Right: N kò lọ
- Why: Mo kò lọ is not the usual form. COERLL (page 35 and its pronoun table) and Wiktionary ("n") show n before kò in negative sentences. In speech, Mi ò lọ is also common.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): ___ kò lọ. (I did not go.)
  - Choices: N | Mo | Ó
  - Answer: N
- Q2 (blank): A ___ lọ. (We did not go.)
  - Choices: ò | ń | jẹ́
  - Answer: ò
- Q3 (blank): ___ lọ. (He did not go.)
  - Choices: Kò | Ó | Mo
  - Answer: Kò
- Q4 (blank): N kò ___ ẹja. (I did not eat fish.)
  - Choices: jẹ | mu | kà
  - Answer: jẹ
- Q5 (blank): A ò ___ omi. (We did not drink water.)
  - Choices: mu | jẹ | ra
  - Answer: mu
- Q6 (pick): Which is the usual way to say "I did not go"?
  - Choices: Mo kò lọ | N kò lọ | Ń kò lọ
  - Answer: N kò lọ
- Q7 (order): We did not drink water
  - Answer: A ò mu omi
- Q8 (order): I did not eat fish
  - Answer: N kò jẹ ẹja

#### Ongoing action with ń (`b-3`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Mo jẹun
- Right: Mo ń jẹun
- Why: To say "I am eating", you need ń. COERLL notes that without ń, the verb points to the past, so Mo jẹun means "I ate".

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Mo ___ jẹun. (I am eating.)
  - Choices: ń | kò | jẹ́
  - Answer: ń
- Q2 (blank): A ń ___. (We are singing.)
  - Choices: kọrin | sùn | rìn
  - Answer: kọrin
- Q3 (blank): Kí ni o ń ___? (What are you doing?)
  - Choices: ṣe | mu | lọ
  - Answer: ṣe
- Q4 (pick): Which means "I am eating"?
  - Choices: Mo jẹun | Mo ń jẹun | Mo ń lọ
  - Answer: Mo ń jẹun
- Q5 (pick): In Adé ń mu omi, what does ń tell you?
  - Choices: The action is going on | The action is already done | The action will happen later
  - Answer: The action is going on
- Q6 (order): We are singing
  - Answer: A ń kọrin
- Q7 (order): What are you doing?
  - Answer: Kí ni o ń ṣe
- Q8 (order): Adé is drinking water
  - Answer: Adé ń mu omi

#### Questions: ṣé and kí ni (`b-4`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: O fẹ́ jẹun ṣé?
- Right: Ṣé o fẹ́ jẹun?
- Why: Putting ṣé at the end is not the usual form. Wiktionary ("ṣe") describes ṣé as placed before a sentence, and COERLL (page 41) puts it first in Ṣé o fẹ́ jẹun?

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): ___ o fẹ́ jẹun? (Do you want to eat?)
  - Choices: Ṣé | Kí ni | Èyí ni
  - Answer: Ṣé
- Q2 (blank): ___ ni o fẹ́? (What do you want?)
  - Choices: Kí | Ṣé | Níbo
  - Answer: Kí
- Q3 (blank): ___ ni èyí? (Who is this?)
  - Choices: Ta | Kí | Ṣé
  - Answer: Ta
- Q4 (blank): ___, mo fẹ́ omi. (Yes, I want water.)
  - Choices: Bẹ́ẹ̀ ni | Rárá | Ṣé
  - Answer: Bẹ́ẹ̀ ni
- Q5 (pick): Which is the usual way to ask "Do you want to eat?"
  - Choices: O fẹ́ jẹun ṣé? | Ṣé o fẹ́ jẹun? | O ṣé fẹ́ jẹun?
  - Answer: Ṣé o fẹ́ jẹun?
- Q6 (pick): Which one is a yes or no question?
  - Choices: Kí ni o fẹ́? | Kí ni orúkọ rẹ? | Ṣé o sùn dáadáa?
  - Answer: Ṣé o sùn dáadáa?
- Q7 (order): Do you want water?
  - Answer: Ṣé o fẹ́ omi
- Q8 (order): Are you eating?
  - Answer: Ṣé o ń jẹun

### Intermediate

#### Done, will, and usually: ti, máa, yóò (`i-1`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Mo yóò lọ
- Right: Èmi yóò lọ
- Why: yóò needs a long pronoun before it, so use èmi, not mo. With mo, use máa instead: Mo máa lọ.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Mo ___ jẹun. (I have eaten.)
  - Choices: ti | ń | máa
  - Answer: ti
- Q2 (blank): Èmi ___ lọ. (I will go.)
  - Choices: yóò | ti | ń
  - Answer: yóò
- Q3 (blank): Mo ___ ń jẹun. (I usually eat.)
  - Choices: máa | ti | yóò
  - Answer: máa
- Q4 (blank): Mo máa wá ___. (I will come tomorrow.)
  - Choices: lọ́la | lánàá | nísinsìnyí
  - Answer: lọ́la
- Q5 (pick): Which means "I usually eat"?
  - Choices: Mo ti jẹun | Mo ń jẹun | Mo máa ń jẹun
  - Answer: Mo máa ń jẹun
- Q6 (order): I have eaten
  - Answer: Mo ti jẹun
- Q7 (order): I will buy a book today
  - Answer: Èmi yóò ra ìwé lónìí
- Q8 (order): I usually eat in the morning
  - Answer: Mo máa ń jẹun ní àárọ̀

#### Possession: the owner comes second (`i-2`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: bàbá ilé
- Right: ilé bàbá
- Why: For "father's house", the house comes first and the father second. With the order swapped, the phrase reads as "the father of the house", close to baálé, head of the household.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Ilé ___ (the king's house)
  - Choices: ọba | ilé | bàbá
  - Answer: ọba
- Q2 (blank): Ọkọ̀ ___ (our vehicle)
  - Choices: wa | mi | wọn
  - Answer: wa
- Q3 (blank): Aṣọ ___ (your clothes, one person)
  - Choices: rẹ | wa | wọn
  - Answer: rẹ
- Q4 (blank): Ilé bàbá ___ (my father's house)
  - Choices: mi | wa | rẹ
  - Answer: mi
- Q5 (pick): Which means "father's house"?
  - Choices: bàbá ilé | ilé bàbá | ilé ọba
  - Answer: ilé bàbá
- Q6 (pick): In a phrase like ilé ọba, where does the owner go?
  - Choices: Right after the thing owned | Before the thing owned | At the end of the sentence
  - Answer: Right after the thing owned
- Q7 (order): My father's vehicle
  - Answer: Ọkọ̀ bàbá mi
- Q8 (order): Adé's father's house
  - Answer: Ilé bàbá Adé

#### Words for people: olù- and oní- (`i-3`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: oníẹja
- Right: ẹlẹ́ja
- Why: oní- changes to match a noun that starts with ẹ. The usual word for a fish seller is ẹlẹ́ja.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): oní + ẹja (fish) becomes ___ (fish seller).
  - Choices: ẹlẹ́ja | oníẹja | onílẹja
  - Answer: ẹlẹ́ja
- Q2 (blank): Landlord: ___lé
  - Choices: oní | olù | ẹlẹ́
  - Answer: oní
- Q3 (blank): oní + owó (money) becomes ___ (rich person).
  - Choices: olówó | oníowó | ẹlẹ́wó
  - Answer: olówó
- Q4 (blank): olù + gbọ́ (hear) is olùgbọ́, one who ___.
  - Choices: hears | sells | owns
  - Answer: hears
- Q5 (pick): What happens to the two i sounds when oní meets ilé?
  - Choices: They merge into one high í | The n is dropped | Nothing changes
  - Answer: They merge into one high í
- Q6 (pick): Which prefix goes before a verb?
  - Choices: oní- | olù- | ẹlẹ́-
  - Answer: olù-
- Q7 (order): My teacher's house
  - Answer: Ilé olùkọ́ mi
- Q8 (order): My landlord's vehicle
  - Answer: Ọkọ̀ onílé mi

#### This, that, and plurals (`i-4`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: ọmọ àwọn
- Right: àwọn ọmọ
- Why: ọmọ àwọn is not the usual form for "children". COERLL (page 55) and Wiktionary ("awọn") put àwọn before the noun. The noun does not change and takes no ending.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Ọmọ ___ (this child)
  - Choices: yìí | yẹn | àwọn
  - Answer: yìí
- Q2 (blank): Ọmọ ___ (that child)
  - Choices: yẹn | yìí | àwọn
  - Answer: yẹn
- Q3 (blank): ___ ọmọ (children)
  - Choices: Àwọn | Yìí | Yẹn
  - Answer: Àwọn
- Q4 (blank): Ìwé ___? (Which book?)
  - Choices: wo | yìí | mi
  - Answer: wo
- Q5 (pick): In ọmọ yìí, where does yìí go?
  - Choices: After the noun | Before the noun | At the start of the sentence
  - Answer: After the noun
- Q6 (pick): Which means "these children"?
  - Choices: ọmọ yẹn | àwọn ọmọ wọ̀nyí | ọmọ àwọn
  - Answer: àwọn ọmọ wọ̀nyí
- Q7 (order): That big house
  - Answer: Ilé ńlá yẹn
- Q8 (order): These children
  - Answer: Àwọn ọmọ wọ̀nyí

### Advanced

#### When two vowels meet (`a-1`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: rẹja (for "to see fish")
- Right: rẹ́ja
- Why: When rí loses its vowel, its high tone stays. COERLL (page 245) writes rí ẹja as rẹ́ja. Without the high mark, rẹja is the form Wikipedia gives for ra ẹja, to buy fish.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): ra ẹran (to buy meat) is said ___.
  - Choices: rẹran | raẹran | rẹ́ran
  - Answer: rẹran
- Q2 (blank): rí ẹja (to see fish) is said ___ja.
  - Choices: rẹ́ | ra | rí
  - Answer: rẹ́
- Q3 (blank): ta iṣu (to sell yams) is said ___.
  - Choices: taṣu | taiṣu | tasu
  - Answer: taṣu
- Q4 (blank): ra ẹyin (to buy eggs) is said ___.
  - Choices: rẹyin | raẹyin | rẹ́yin
  - Answer: rẹyin
- Q5 (blank): ta ẹyin (to sell eggs) is said ___.
  - Choices: tẹyin | taẹyin | tẹ́yin
  - Answer: tẹyin
- Q6 (pick): How is kú ilé (welcome into the house) said?
  - Choices: kúulé | kúlé | kílé
  - Answer: kúulé
- Q7 (order): I did not buy eggs
  - Answer: N kò rẹyin
- Q8 (order): I did not buy meat
  - Answer: N kò rẹran

#### Verbal nouns: lílọ, jíjẹ, kíkà (`a-2`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: kikà
- Right: kíkà
- Why: The added i always has a high tone, whatever the verb's own tone. Inkelas and Zoll (citing Kawu) describe it as a high-tone í. kikà is not the usual form.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): jẹ (eat) → ___jẹ (eating)
  - Choices: jí | jẹ | lí
  - Answer: jí
- Q2 (blank): kà (read) → ___ (reading)
  - Choices: kíkà | kàkà | kíkí
  - Answer: kíkà
- Q3 (blank): gbé (carry) → ___gbé (carrying)
  - Choices: gbí | bí | gí
  - Answer: gbí
- Q4 (blank): rìn (walk) → ___ (walking)
  - Choices: rírìn | rìnrìn | ríí
  - Answer: rírìn
- Q5 (blank): sùn (sleep) → ___ (sleeping)
  - Choices: sísùn | sùnsùn | sísí
  - Answer: sísùn
- Q6 (pick): lọ (go) becomes which verbal noun?
  - Choices: lílọ | lọlọ | jíjẹ
  - Answer: lílọ
- Q7 (pick): What tone does the added í always carry?
  - Choices: High | Low | The same as the verb
  - Answer: High
- Q8 (order): Going to school
  - Answer: Ilé-ìwé lílọ

#### Serial verbs (`a-3`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Olú mú ìwé kò wá ilé
- Right: Olú kò mú ìwé wá ilé
- Why: Olú did not bring a book home. kò goes before the first verb, never between the verbs. Ariyo (2025, page 53) marks the first version as ungrammatical.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Ó mú ìwé ___. (He brought the book.)
  - Choices: wá | lọ | kò
  - Answer: wá
- Q2 (blank): Ó gbé ìwé ___. (He took the book away.)
  - Choices: lọ | wá | jẹ
  - Answer: lọ
- Q3 (blank): Olú ___ mú ìwé wá ilé. (Olú did not bring a book home.)
  - Choices: kò | ń | ni
  - Answer: kò
- Q4 (blank): Mo mú omi wá ___. (I brought water home.)
  - Choices: ilé | ọ̀bẹ | ẹran
  - Answer: ilé
- Q5 (pick): Which means "Olú took the knife to cut meat"?
  - Choices: Olú mú ọ̀bẹ gé ẹran | Olú gé ọ̀bẹ mú ẹran | Olú mú ẹran gé ọ̀bẹ
  - Answer: Olú mú ọ̀bẹ gé ẹran
- Q6 (pick): Where does kò go in a serial verb sentence?
  - Choices: Before the first verb | Between the two verbs | At the end
  - Answer: Before the first verb
- Q7 (order): I brought water home
  - Answer: Mo mú omi wá ilé
- Q8 (order): Olú took the knife to cut meat
  - Answer: Olú mú ọ̀bẹ gé ẹran

#### Relative clauses with tí (`a-4`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: ọmọ tí lọ
- Right: ọmọ tí ó lọ
- Why: The child is the one who went, so ó stays after tí. Lawal (1987) shows that a clause about the doer keeps this pronoun. ọmọ tí lọ is not the usual form.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): ọmọ ___ ó lọ (the child who went)
  - Choices: tí | ni | àti
  - Answer: tí
- Q2 (blank): adìyẹ tí mo ___ (the chicken that I bought)
  - Choices: rà | ra | tà
  - Answer: rà
- Q3 (blank): ọkùnrin tí ___ ra aṣọ (the man who bought the cloth)
  - Choices: ó | mo | a
  - Answer: ó
- Q4 (blank): ìwé tí mo ___ (the book that I read)
  - Choices: kà | jẹ | wá
  - Answer: kà
- Q5 (blank): obìnrin tí ó ___ (the woman who came)
  - Choices: wá | kà | jẹ
  - Answer: wá
- Q6 (pick): Which means "the house that Bọ́lá lives in"?
  - Choices: ilé tí Bọ́lá ń gbé | ilé Bọ́lá tí ń gbé | ilé tí ń gbé Bọ́lá
  - Answer: ilé tí Bọ́lá ń gbé
- Q7 (order): The food that I ate
  - Answer: Oúnjẹ tí mo jẹ
- Q8 (order): The book that I read
  - Answer: Ìwé tí mo kà

### Mastery

#### Words that join into one (`m-1`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: ní ọ̀la
- Right: lọ́la
- Why: Tomorrow is written as one word. Wiktionary gives lọ́la, and Wikipedia notes that Yoruba spelling follows speech and does not split contracted words. ní ọ̀la is the unfused form, not the usual way to write it.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): ní + ọ̀la fuse into ___ (tomorrow).
  - Choices: lọ́la | nọ́la | ní ọ̀la
  - Answer: lọ́la
- Q2 (blank): ní + ilé fuse into ___ (at home).
  - Choices: nílé | lílé | ní ilé
  - Answer: nílé
- Q3 (blank): tí + ó fuse into ___.
  - Choices: tó | tí ó | ní
  - Answer: tó
- Q4 (blank): ní + ọjà fuse into ___ (at the market).
  - Choices: lọ́jà | nọ́jà | ní ọjà
  - Answer: lọ́jà
- Q5 (blank): ní + àná fuse into ___ (yesterday).
  - Choices: lánàá | nánàá | ní àná
  - Answer: lánàá
- Q6 (pick): What happens to the n of ní in lọ́la?
  - Choices: It becomes l | It stays n | It becomes a high tone
  - Answer: It becomes l
- Q7 (pick): What happens to the two i sounds in ní + ilé?
  - Choices: They merge into one high í | Both stay | The n is dropped
  - Answer: They merge into one high í
- Q8 (blank): lónìí (today) is written as ___ word.
  - Choices: one | two | three
  - Answer: one

#### Focus with ni (`m-2`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Adé ni ra ìwé
- Right: Adé ni ó ra ìwé
- Why: Adé is the one who bought it, so ó follows ni. Jones (2006, page 144) says this ó is obligatory when the subject is focused, in the pattern shown here.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Adé ___ ó ra ìwé. (It was Adé who bought the book.)
  - Choices: ni | kò | ń
  - Answer: ni
- Q2 (blank): Akin ni ó ___. (It was Akin who went.)
  - Choices: lọ | ra | gbé
  - Answer: lọ
- Q3 (blank): Ìwé ni Adé ___. (It was a book that Adé bought.)
  - Choices: rà | ra | lọ
  - Answer: rà
- Q4 (blank): ___ ni ó lọ? (Who went?)
  - Choices: Ta | Kí | Ṣé
  - Answer: Ta
- Q5 (blank): Bùkọ́lá ni ó ___ aṣọ. (It was Bùkọ́lá who bought clothes.)
  - Choices: ra | rà | lọ
  - Answer: ra
- Q6 (pick): Which means "It was not Olú who carried the stick"?
  - Choices: Olú kọ́ ni ó gbé igi | Olú ni ó gbé igi | Olú kò gbé igi
  - Answer: Olú kọ́ ni ó gbé igi
- Q7 (order): It was Adé who bought the book
  - Answer: Adé ni ó ra ìwé
- Q8 (order): What did Adé buy?
  - Answer: Kí ni Adé rà

#### Complex sentences: if and because (`m-3`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Bí òjò rọ̀
- Right: Bí òjò bá rọ̀
- Why: COERLL (page 89) lists tí ... bá for "if", and its song says Bí o bá kàwé rẹ. Wiktionary shows that bí without bá can mean "as soon as". So for "if", bá is the usual form.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): ___ òjò bá rọ̀. (If it rains.)
  - Choices: Bí | Nítorí | Ṣé
  - Answer: Bí
- Q2 (blank): Bí o ___ kàwé rẹ. (If you read your books.)
  - Choices: bá | ti | ń
  - Answer: bá
- Q3 (blank): ___ pé òjò rọ̀. (Because it rained.)
  - Choices: Nítorí | Bí | Ṣé
  - Answer: Nítorí
- Q4 (blank): Mo fẹ́ lọ, ___ òjò ń rọ̀. (I want to go, but it is raining.)
  - Choices: ṣùgbọ́n | àti | bí
  - Answer: ṣùgbọ́n
- Q5 (pick): Which means "we will not go"?
  - Choices: A ò níí lọ | A ò lọ | A máa lọ
  - Answer: A ò níí lọ
- Q6 (pick): In an if-clause, where does bá go?
  - Choices: Right before the verb | At the end of the sentence | Before bí
  - Answer: Right before the verb
- Q7 (order): If it rains, we will not go
  - Answer: Bí òjò bá rọ̀ a ò níí lọ
- Q8 (order): I want to go, but it is raining
  - Answer: Mo fẹ́ lọ ṣùgbọ́n òjò ń rọ̀

#### Proverbs (òwe) (`m-4`)

Common mistake:
- Wrong: Ọmọ tí a kò kọ́ yóò ta ilé
- Right: Ọmọ tí a kò kọ́ ni yóò gbé ilé tí a kọ́ tà
- Why: The first is a paraphrase, not the proverb. It drops ni, gbé and "the house that is built", and with it the play on kọ́ (teach) and kọ́ (build) that Afolayan (2021) describes. Quote proverbs word for word.

Questions:
- Q1 (blank): Ọmọ tí a kò ___ (the untaught child)
  - Choices: kọ́ | tà | rí
  - Answer: kọ́
- Q2 (blank): Ọmọ tí a kò kọ́ ni yóò gbé ilé tí a kọ́ ___.
  - Choices: tà | kọ́ | gbé
  - Answer: tà
- Q3 (blank): Òní l'a ___, ọba òkè l'ó rọ́la.
  - Choices: rí | tà | kọ́
  - Answer: rí
- Q4 (blank): Ọmọ tí a kò kọ́ ___ yóò gbé ilé tí a kọ́ tà. (The word that puts the spotlight on the child.)
  - Choices: ni | tí | kò
  - Answer: ni
- Q5 (blank): Bí iná bá jó ___, màjàlà á fò wá sílé. (When a fire burns in the field, ...)
  - Choices: lóko | nílé | lọ́jà
  - Answer: lóko
- Q6 (pick): What does Ọmọ tí a kò kọ́ ni yóò gbé ilé tí a kọ́ tà mean?
  - Choices: The child who is not taught will sell the house that is built | The child who is not taught will build a house | A house that is not built cannot be sold
  - Answer: The child who is not taught will sell the house that is built
- Q7 (pick): What does Bí ewé bá pẹ́ lára ọṣẹ, á dọṣẹ teach?
  - Choices: People who spend long together take on each other's ways | Leaves make the best soap | Soap takes a long time to make
  - Answer: People who spend long together take on each other's ways
- Q8 (order): The child who is not taught
  - Answer: Ọmọ tí a kò kọ́
